---
name: aster-code-review
description: Review code against Asterinas's persona-keyed coding guidelines and write a Markdown review file. Use when asked to review a Git change (diff mode) or a set of target files (files mode) for defects, or inside a write-test-review loop.

# aster-code-review

Review code against Asterinas's coding guidelines
— the persona-keyed pages under `book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/`
— and write one Markdown review file.
The review is **recall-first**: miss as few real defects as possible.
It makes both objective calls (undeniable bugs) and subjective calls (guideline violations, each grounded in a cited rule).

There are two review modes, both **anchored at the current checkout (HEAD)**:

- **`diff <base>`** — a Git change:
  the **commit series** the branch adds over its merge-base with `<base>`
  — each commit's message *and* its diff,
  so the review covers per-commit intent and commit hygiene, not just the net change.
  (Commit first; uncommitted edits are not reviewed in diff mode.)
- **`files <path[:lines] ...>`**
  — target code: the working-tree contents of the named files,
  optionally narrowed to line ranges.

To review at a specific commit, check it out first (it becomes HEAD).

This skill is agent-agnostic (Claude Code and Codex).
Only step 3's spawn primitive differs between agents;
everything else is identical.

## Interface

However the skill is triggered,
it receives ONE raw argument string that begins with a mode word:

```
diff   <base>              <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
files  <path[:lines] ...>  <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
```

- `<base>` (diff) — **required**,
  any ref or SHA;
  reviews the commit series `merge-base(<base>, HEAD)..HEAD` (each commit's message + diff).
- `<path[:lines] ...>` (files) — **required**, one or more targets in the working tree.
  A target is a path, optionally `path:N-M` or `path:N-M,K-L` (1-based, inclusive);
  repeat a path to add ranges.
  Wrap a path in double quotes to allow spaces or colons (`"a: b.rs":10-20`).
- `<output>` — **required, last positional** (`cp src... dest` style);
  refuse to overwrite unless `--overwrite`.
- `--overwrite` — replace the output file if it already exists.
- `--per-persona-context` — `yes`: fan out one isolated agent per persona (best recall).
  `no`: one combined agent reviews all personas (cheaper, lower recall).
  `auto` (default) currently resolves to `yes`;
  it will become a benchmark-driven heuristic.
  Controls step 3 only.

**Do not pre-split the argument string yourself.**
Pass it verbatim, as a single quoted argument,
to `resolve_target.sh` (step 1)
— the script self-tokenizes (whitespace-split except inside double quotes),
which keeps the parse deterministic and identical across agents.

## Pipeline

Run these steps in order.
Steps 1 and 5 are deterministic scripts;
steps 6–8 use the model.
Create one fresh temporary directory for the whole run with `mktemp -d`
and put the resolved input, meta file, pass prompts, and fragment directory under it.
Do not use fixed paths such as `/tmp/aster-review-frags`;
stale fragments are worse than a hard failure.

The helper scripts live in the skill's own `scripts/` directory,
at `.agents/skills/aster-code-review/scripts/` inside the repository under review.
**Invoke every script by its absolute path**
— the working directory is the code under review (the repo root), *not* the skill directory,
so a bare `scripts/…` is not found (notably on Codex, which runs commands from the repo root; do not `cd` into the skill dir either — `resolve_target.sh` needs the repo as its cwd).
Shells do not persist between commands,
so **set `SKILL` at the start of each command that uses it** (or inline the `$(git rev-parse …)`):

```sh
SKILL="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.agents/skills/aster-code-review"; "$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'
```

The steps below write `$SKILL/scripts/…` as shorthand for that absolute path.

1. **Resolve the target.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'` prints the canonical review input
   — the commit series (each commit's message + diff) in `diff` mode,
   annotated file excerpts in `files` mode;
   save it to a temp file.
   Run it again with `--meta` first (`"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" --meta '<raw args>'`) to get
   `mode=`, `base=`/`files=`, `head=`, `branch=`, `output=`, `overwrite=`, `per_persona_context=`;
   add `date=` (today) and an optional `title=` to make the meta file.
   Pass the raw argument string as a single quoted argument
   so the script's tokenizer sees it intact.
2. **Activate personas.**
   Pick which of the five personas run,
   from the reviewed paths — changed paths in `diff` mode,
   named paths in `files` mode (see *Activation*).
3. **Fan out.**
   Spawn the persona passes (see *Spawning*).
   With `per_persona_context` = `yes` or `auto` (the default),
   run **one isolated PASS per activated persona** — best recall.
   With `no`, run **one combined PASS** over all activated personas in a single context
   — cheaper, lower recall.
   Build each pass's prompt deterministically with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
   — one persona for fan-out, all activated personas for combined
   — and spawn the sub-agent with **that exact text**.
   Each pass returns a JSON array of comments;
   file each under its persona's `<fragdir>/<persona>.json` (the comment's `persona` field says which),
   so step 5 is unchanged.
   The `<fragdir>` must be freshly created for this review,
   and no pass may read or reuse a fragment from an earlier review.
4. **Collect** the per-persona JSON fragments.
5. **Assemble.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/assemble_review.sh" [--overwrite] <meta> <fragdir> <output>` performs the deterministic merge (group by persona in fixed order, sort by file→line, drop exact duplicates *within a persona*, write frontmatter, leave a `<!-- SUMMARY -->` placeholder).
   Pass `--overwrite` only if the user gave it;
   otherwise the script refuses to clobber an existing `<output>`.
6. **Verify** (see *Verification*).
7. **Consolidate** (see *Consolidation*).
8. **Summary.**
   Replace `<!-- SUMMARY -->` with a constructive summary over the final comments:
   what the code does well, the top issues ranked by severity,
   any structural recommendations.

## Activation

A persona runs unless the reviewed paths *provably* contain nothing in its remit (reviewed paths = changed paths in `diff` mode, named paths in `files` mode):

- **maintainability, development, security** — any reviewed code.
- **hardware** — a reviewed path is assembly (`*.S`, `*.asm`),
  an architecture directory (`.../arch/...`), or contains `asm!` / `global_asm!`.
- **documentation** — a reviewed path is `book/`, any `*.md`,
  syscall-coverage files (`*.scml`), or a user-facing API surface (a syscall or kernel parameter).

Activation is path-based and deterministic;
do not use a model to triage (a wrongly-skipped persona is a silent recall hole).

## Pass contract

The shared reviewer contract
— the review methodology and the JSON comment schema every pass must follow
— lives in [`scripts/pass_contract.md`](scripts/pass_contract.md),
inlined as the **stable head** of every pass prompt by `build_pass_prompt.sh` (step 3).
Do not paraphrase it into the prompt yourself:
retyping it breaks the byte-identical prefix the prompt cache relies on (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).
A pass reads only the persona block(s) it is given (selective exposure),
reviews the **REVIEW INPUT** at the foot of the prompt,
and returns a JSON array of comments per that schema.

## Spawning a pass

Build every pass prompt with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
and spawn the sub-agent with its **exact** output
— only a script keeps the prompt's stable head (contract + guideline) byte-identical across passes and reviews,
which is what lets the prompt cache reuse it (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).

**Default (`per_persona_context` = `yes`/`auto`)**
— one pass per activated persona,
each in a CLEAN context with only its own persona block (selective exposure):

- **Claude Code** — spawn a Task sub-agent per persona.
- **Codex** — write each prompt to a file and run the pass with
  `--output-last-message`/`-o`, for example:

  ```sh
  timeout "${ACR_PASS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS:-600}" \
    codex exec -C "$repo" --sandbox read-only \
      -o "$fragdir/$persona.json" \
      - < "$promptdir/$persona.txt" \
      > "$logdir/$persona.log" 2>&1
  ```

  Do **not** redirect `codex exec` stdout to the fragment file;
  stdout contains session logs as well as the final answer.
  The fragment file must contain only the model's final JSON array.
  Validate every fragment with `python3 -m json.tool` before assembly.
  If a pass times out or validation fails, fail closed instead of assembling a
  review from missing, logged, or stale fragments.

**Never spawn a pass by re-running `aster_code_review.sh` / `run_agent.sh`,
nor by re-issuing the skill's own arguments** (e.g. `codex exec … diff <base> <out>`).
A pass is a *reviewer* invocation whose input is the `build_pass_prompt.sh` text
— not another run of this skill.
Re-entering the launcher spawns another orchestrator that spawns another … , an infinite fork bomb;
the launcher now refuses it (`ACR_AGENT_RUNNING`).

Passes are independent;
order does not matter.
Collect each pass's JSON.

**Combined (`per_persona_context` = `no`)**
— build ONE prompt with all activated personas (`"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input> <p1> <p2> ...`)
and run a single pass;
it reviews through every lens at once and tags each comment with its `persona`.
The input and spawn overhead are paid once,
so it is cheaper
— but a larger shared context dilutes focus,
so it finds fewer defects.
The default stays fan-out:
recall comes first,
and `no` is an explicit, opt-in concession.

## Verification (step 6)

For each comment, isolate the key premise it rests on
— especially an external-system fact (Linux/POSIX behaviour, the System V ABI, Rust semantics).
Try to **refute** it:
re-read the cited code and consult an authoritative source.
For guideline findings,
verify against the persona template and the inlined/bundled guideline root used to build the pass prompt.
Do not retract a finding merely because the historical worktree's `book/` copy has older or narrower wording
when the persona template explicitly instructs the check.
Assign a verdict:

- **confirmed** — keep the comment unchanged.
- **uncertain** — keep it, but prefix `problem` with `(unverified) `.
- **refuted** — remove the comment,
  and append it to a `## Retracted by verification` list at the foot of the file with a one-line reason.

Remove **only** on confident refutation;
an unsure check is `uncertain`, not `refuted`.
This is the only step that may remove a comment, and only false positives.

## Consolidation (step 7)

Find clusters of comments that share one root cause or one fix
(e.g. several manual lock/unlock pairs that all want RAII).
For each cluster, write a single unified fix
and repoint each member's `**Fix.**` at it ("Shared with the other `raii` comments: …").
**Never remove a comment**
— every symptom stays at its own location.

## Output format

The assembled file is:
frontmatter (`date`, `mode`, `base` or `files`, `head`, `branch`, optional `title`),
a `# Summary`,
then a `## <section>` per persona in the order **Maintainability, Correctness, Security, Hardware, Documentation** (Correctness is the Development persona's section).

 succeeded in 0ms:
Each comment is a `### \`file\` line N` subsection:
a quoted ` ```diff ` snippet,
then a prose line `` `<grounding>` (<severity>): <problem> ``,
then a `**Fix.**` paragraph.
Do not restructure what the script emits;
only fill the summary and apply the verify/consolidate edits.

===== kernel/src/syscall/chdir.rs =====
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	use super::SyscallReturn;
     4	use crate::{
     5	    fs::{
     6	        file::{
     7	            InodeType,
     8	            file_table::{RawFileDesc, get_file_fast},
     9	        },
    10	        vfs::path::FsPath,
    11	    },
    12	    prelude::*,
    13	    syscall::constants::MAX_FILENAME_LEN,
    14	};
    15	
    16	pub fn sys_chdir(path_ptr: Vaddr, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    17	    let path_name = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_ptr, MAX_FILENAME_LEN)?;
    18	    debug!("path = {:?}", path_name);
    19	
    20	    let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
    21	    let mut path_resolver = fs_ref.resolver().write();
    22	    let path = {
    23	        let path_name = path_name.to_string_lossy();
    24	        if path_name.is_empty() {
    25	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOENT, "path is empty");
    26	        }
    27	        let fs_path = FsPath::try_from(path_name.as_ref())?;
    28	        path_resolver.lookup(&fs_path)?
    29	    };
    30	    if path.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
    31	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "must be directory");
    32	    }
    33	    path_resolver.set_cwd(path);
    34	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    35	}
    36	
    37	pub fn sys_fchdir(raw_fd: RawFileDesc, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    38	    debug!("raw_fd = {}", raw_fd);
    39	
    40	    let path = {
    41	        let mut file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table_mut();
    42	        let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, raw_fd.try_into()?);
    43	        file.as_inode_handle_or_err()?.path().clone()
    44	    };
    45	    if path.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
    46	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "must be directory");
    47	    }
    48	    let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
    49	    fs_ref.resolver().write().set_cwd(path);
    50	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    51	}

===== kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs =====
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	use super::SyscallReturn;
     4	use crate::{
     5	    fs::{
     6	        file::file_table::{RawFileDesc, get_file_fast},
     7	        utils::PATH_MAX,
     8	        vfs::path::{AT_FDCWD, EmptyPathStr, FsPath},
     9	    },
    10	    prelude::*,
    11	    process::{Gid, Uid},
    12	};
    13	
    14	pub fn sys_fchown(raw_fd: RawFileDesc, uid: i32, gid: i32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    15	    debug!("raw_fd = {}, uid = {}, gid = {}", raw_fd, uid, gid);
    16	
    17	    let uid = to_optional_id(uid, Uid::new)?;
    18	    let gid = to_optional_id(gid, Gid::new)?;
    19	    if uid.is_none() && gid.is_none() {
    20	        return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0));
    21	    }
    22	
    23	    let mut file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table_mut();
    24	    let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, raw_fd.try_into()?);
    25	    let path = file.path();
    26	    if let Some(uid) = uid {
    27	        path.set_owner(uid)?;
    28	    }
    29	    if let Some(gid) = gid {
    30	        path.set_group(gid)?;
    31	    }
    32	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    33	}
    34	
    35	pub fn sys_chown(path_ptr: Vaddr, uid: i32, gid: i32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    36	    sys_fchownat(AT_FDCWD, path_ptr, uid, gid, 0, ctx)
    37	}
    38	
    39	pub fn sys_lchown(path_ptr: Vaddr, uid: i32, gid: i32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    40	    sys_fchownat(
    41	        AT_FDCWD,
    42	        path_ptr,
    43	        uid,
    44	        gid,
    45	        ChownFlags::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW.bits(),
    46	        ctx,
    47	    )
    48	}
    49	
    50	pub fn sys_fchownat(
    51	    dirfd: RawFileDesc,
    52	    path_ptr: Vaddr,
    53	    uid: i32,
    54	    gid: i32,
    55	    flags: u32,
    56	    ctx: &Context,
    57	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    58	    let path_name = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_ptr, PATH_MAX)?;
    59	    let flags = ChownFlags::from_bits(flags)
    60	        .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid flags"))?;
    61	    debug!(
    62	        "dirfd = {}, path = {:?}, uid = {}, gid = {}, flags = {:?}",
    63	        dirfd, path_name, uid, gid, flags
    64	    );
    65	
    66	    if flags.contains(ChownFlags::AT_EMPTY_PATH) && path_name.is_empty() {

# Summary

<!-- SUMMARY -->

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/syscall/chdir.rs` line 24

> ```diff
> if path_name.is_empty() {
>     return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOENT, "path is empty");
> }
> ```

`dry` (minor): The explicit empty-path check duplicates `FsPath::try_from`, which already applies `EmptyPathStr::Reject` and returns `ENOENT`; keeping two copies allows validation and error messages to drift.

**Fix.** Remove the local `path_name.is_empty()` branch and let `FsPath::try_from` own empty-path handling.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 66

> ```diff
> if flags.contains(ChownFlags::AT_EMPTY_PATH) && path_name.is_empty() {
>     return sys_fchown(dirfd, uid, gid, ctx);
> }
> ```

`dry` (minor): `FsPath::from_fd_at(..., EmptyPathStr::AllowIfFlag(...))` already converts an allowed empty path into an fd-targeted path, and `PathResolver::lookup` resolves it; this early dispatch duplicates that shared normalization and bypasses it.

**Fix.** Remove the special-case return and resolve the path through `FsPath::from_fd_at` and the resolver uniformly.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 99

> ```diff
> fn to_optional_id<T>(id: i32, f: impl Fn(u32) -> T) -> Result<Option<T>> {
>     let id = if id >= 0 {
>         Some(f(id as u32))
> ```

`closure-fn-suffix` (minor): `f` is a callable `impl Fn(u32) -> T` parameter, but its name does not signal that it is a function as required by the local Rust naming convention.

**Fix.** Rename the parameter to `id_fn` and call `id_fn(id as u32)`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 103

> ```diff
> // If the owner or group is specified as -1, then that ID is not changed.
> ```

`backtick-identifiers` (nit): The explanatory comment uses the sentinel literal `-1` as plain prose, making the code value less visually distinct.

**Fix.** Format the sentinel as code: `// If the owner or group is specified as `-1`, then that ID is not changed.`

### `kernel/src/syscall/chroot.rs` line 21

> ```diff
> if path_name.is_empty() {
>     return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOENT, "path is empty");
> }
> ```

`dry` (minor): The explicit empty-path check duplicates `FsPath::try_from`'s `EmptyPathStr::Reject` policy, creating a second implementation that can drift from the shared resolver contract.

**Fix.** Remove the local check and rely on `FsPath::try_from` to reject empty paths.

### `kernel/src/syscall/stat.rs` line 61

> ```diff
> if flags.contains(StatFlags::AT_EMPTY_PATH) && filename.is_empty() {
>     return sys_fstat(dirfd, stat_buf_ptr, ctx);
> }
> ```

`dry` (minor): `FsPath::from_fd_at(..., EmptyPathStr::AllowIfFlag(...))` already represents an allowed empty path as the target fd, so this separate `sys_fstat` dispatch duplicates the resolver's policy and creates another path for the same operation.

**Fix.** Remove the special-case return and use the normal `FsPath::from_fd_at` plus resolver path for empty and non-empty names.

### `kernel/src/syscall/stat.rs` line 94

> ```diff
> /// This is the x86_64-specific version.
> ```

`backtick-identifiers` (nit): The rustdoc refers to the architecture identifier `x86_64` as plain prose; the corresponding generic-architecture comment below has the same issue.

**Fix.** Format both architecture identifiers as code, for example `This is the `x86_64`-specific version.` and `... except `x86_64`.`

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 14

> ```diff
> pub fn sys_fchown(raw_fd: RawFileDesc, uid: i32, gid: i32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
> fn to_optional_id<T>(id: i32, f: impl Fn(u32) -> T) -> Result<Option<T>> {
> ```

Signed ID interpretation (major): The `chown` arguments use `i32`, so valid unsigned `uid_t`/`gid_t` encodings such as `0x80000000` are interpreted as negative and rejected with `EINVAL`; only `0xffffffff` should mean unchanged.

**Fix.** Use `u32` for `uid` and `gid`, and treat only `u32::MAX` as the unchanged sentinel.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 19

> ```diff
> if uid.is_none() && gid.is_none() {
>     return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0));
> }
> 
> let mut file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table_mut();
> ```

Skipped descriptor validation (major): When both IDs are `-1`, this returns success before validating `raw_fd`; `fchown(-1, -1, -1)` therefore succeeds instead of returning `EBADF`.

**Fix.** Validate and resolve `raw_fd` before applying the no-op return, while skipping the ownership setters when both IDs are unchanged.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 31

> ```diff
> if let Some(uid) = uid {
>     path.set_owner(uid)?;
> }
> if let Some(gid) = gid {
>     path.set_group(gid)?;
> }
> ```

Missing attribute notification (major): Successful `set_owner`/`set_group` calls in `sys_fchown` and `sys_fchownat` do not call `fs::vfs::notify::on_attr_change`, so `inotify` watchers miss ownership changes even though `chmod`, `utimens`, and xattr updates publish `IN_ATTRIB`.

**Fix.** Route both ownership entry points through a helper that calls `fs::vfs::notify::on_attr_change` after each successful ownership mutation, including a partial update before a later setter fails.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 58

> ```diff
> let path_name = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_ptr, PATH_MAX)?;
> let flags = ChownFlags::from_bits(flags)
> ```

Incorrect NULL handling (major): With `AT_EMPTY_PATH`, a `NULL` pathname is accepted as the empty path for a real `dirfd`, but `read_cstring(0, ...)` returns `EFAULT` before the flag is parsed, so `fchownat(fd, NULL, ..., AT_EMPTY_PATH)` cannot operate on `fd`.

**Fix.** Parse `flags` before reading the pathname and treat `path_ptr == 0` as an empty pathname only for the permitted `AT_EMPTY_PATH` case; preserve `EFAULT` for other `NULL` inputs.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 72

> ```diff
> let uid = to_optional_id(uid, Uid::new)?;
> let gid = to_optional_id(gid, Gid::new)?;
> if uid.is_none() && gid.is_none() {
>     return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0));
> }
> ```

Skipped path validation (major): When both IDs are `-1`, this returns success before resolving `dirfd` and `path`; `fchownat(AT_FDCWD, "missing", -1, -1, 0)` therefore succeeds instead of returning `ENOENT`.

**Fix.** Resolve and validate the target path before returning the no-op result, then skip only the actual ownership updates.

### `kernel/src/syscall/stat.rs` line 53

> ```diff
> let user_space = ctx.user_space();
> let filename = user_space.read_cstring(filename_ptr, MAX_FILENAME_LEN)?;
> let flags = StatFlags::from_bits(flags)
> ```

Incorrect NULL handling (major): With `AT_EMPTY_PATH`, a `NULL` pathname is accepted as the empty path for a real `dirfd`, but `read_cstring(0, ...)` returns `EFAULT` before the flag is parsed, so `fstatat(fd, NULL, ..., AT_EMPTY_PATH)` fails instead of statting `fd`.

**Fix.** Parse `flags` before reading `filename` and handle `filename_ptr == 0` as the empty pathname only in the permitted `AT_EMPTY_PATH` case.

## Security

### `kernel/src/syscall/chdir.rs` line 30

> ```diff
>     if path.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
>         return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "must be directory");
>     }
>     path_resolver.set_cwd(path);
> ```

Missing execute permission check (major): `lookup` checks search permission on parent directories but does not check the final `path`; this branch checks only its type. Thus `chdir("/private")` succeeds for a directory with no execute permission when its parent is searchable, violating the directory search boundary.

**Fix.** After the type check, require `path.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_EXEC)?` before calling `set_cwd`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chdir.rs` line 45

> ```diff
>     if path.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
>         return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "must be directory");
>     }
>     let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
> ```

Missing execute permission check (major): `sys_fchdir` accepts a directory descriptor, including an `O_PATH` descriptor, and checks only `InodeType::Dir`. An unprivileged caller holding a descriptor for a mode-`000` directory can change its working directory without the required search permission.

**Fix.** Check `path.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_EXEC)?` after verifying that the descriptor refers to a directory and before `set_cwd`.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 19

> ```diff
>     if uid.is_none() && gid.is_none() {
>         return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0));
>     }
> 
>     let mut file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table_mut();
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (minor): The `uid == -1 && gid == -1` fast path returns before `raw_fd.try_into()` and `get_file_fast`, so `fchown` with an invalid descriptor reports success instead of validating the user-controlled resource handle.

**Fix.** Validate and resolve `raw_fd` first, then return the no-op success only after descriptor validation succeeds.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 27

> ```diff
>     if let Some(uid) = uid {
>         path.set_owner(uid)?;
>     }
>     if let Some(gid) = gid {
>         path.set_group(gid)?;
>     }
> ```

Missing authorization (critical): `sys_fchown` mutates ownership through `path.set_owner` and `path.set_group` without checking `CapSet::CHOWN` or the caller's ownership rules. An unprivileged process with an `O_PATH` descriptor can take ownership of a protected file and bypass DAC.

**Fix.** Authorize the operation before mutation using the Linux `chown` rules, then apply the validated owner and group changes atomically where possible.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 72

> ```diff
>     let uid = to_optional_id(uid, Uid::new)?;
>     let gid = to_optional_id(gid, Gid::new)?;
>     if uid.is_none() && gid.is_none() {
>         return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0));
>     }
> 
>     let path = {
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (minor): For a non-empty pathname, `uid == -1 && gid == -1` returns before `FsPath::from_fd_at` and lookup. Calls such as `chown("/missing", -1, -1)` therefore succeed without validating the pathname or `dirfd`.

**Fix.** Resolve and validate the pathname and `dirfd` before applying the all-sentinel no-op return.

### `kernel/src/syscall/chown.rs` line 91

> ```diff
>     if let Some(uid) = uid {
>         path.set_owner(uid)?;
>     }
>     if let Some(gid) = gid {
>         path.set_group(gid)?;
>     }
> ```

Missing authorization (critical): `sys_fchownat`, and therefore `sys_chown` and `sys_lchown`, also call `set_owner` and `set_group` without any authorization check. A caller without `CapSet::CHOWN` can change ownership of any path it can resolve.

**Fix.** Perform the same ownership authorization check used by the descriptor-based entry point before either inode mutation, including the `AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW` path.

## Documentation

### `kernel/src/syscall/chroot.rs` line 31

> ```diff
>     lsm_hooks::on_capable(lsm_hooks::CapableContext::new(
>         ctx.thread_local.borrow_user_ns().as_ref(),
>         ctx.posix_thread,
>         CapSet::SYS_CHROOT,
>     ))?;
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (minor): The added `lsm_hooks::on_capable(...)` check changes the user-visible `chroot()` behavior by requiring `CAP_SYS_CHROOT`, but the Linux Compatibility documentation still only records `chroot(path)` and marks coverage as complete.

**Fix.** Update `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/syscall-flag-coverage/file-systems-and-mount-control/fully_covered.scml` and its documentation to describe the `CAP_SYS_CHROOT` requirement and resulting failure behavior.

    14	pub fn sys_fchown(raw_fd: RawFileDesc, uid: i32, gid: i32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    15	    debug!("raw_fd = {}, uid = {}, gid = {}", raw_fd, uid, gid);
    16	
    17	    let uid = to_optional_id(uid, Uid::new)?;
    18	    let gid = to_optional_id(gid, Gid::new)?;
    19	    if uid.is_none() && gid.is_none() {
    20	        return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0));
    21	    }
    22	
    23	    let mut file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table_mut();
    24	    let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, raw_fd.try_into()?);
    25	    let path = file.path();
    26	    if let Some(uid) = uid {
    27	        path.set_owner(uid)?;
    28	    }
    29	    if let Some(gid) = gid {
    30	        path.set_group(gid)?;
    31	    }
    32	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    33	}

    39	pub fn sys_lchown(path_ptr: Vaddr, uid: i32, gid: i32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    40	    sys_fchownat(
    41	        AT_FDCWD,
    42	        path_ptr,
    43	        uid,
    44	        gid,
    45	        ChownFlags::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW.bits(),
    46	        ctx,
    47	    )
    48	}
    49	
    50	pub fn sys_fchownat(
    51	    dirfd: RawFileDesc,
    52	    path_ptr: Vaddr,
    53	    uid: i32,
    54	    gid: i32,
    55	    flags: u32,
    56	    ctx: &Context,
    57	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    58	    let path_name = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_ptr, PATH_MAX)?;
    59	    let flags = ChownFlags::from_bits(flags)
    60	        .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid flags"))?;
    61	    debug!(
    62	        "dirfd = {}, path = {:?}, uid = {}, gid = {}, flags = {:?}",
    63	        dirfd, path_name, uid, gid, flags
    64	    );
    65	
    66	    if flags.contains(ChownFlags::AT_EMPTY_PATH) && path_name.is_empty() {
    67	        return sys_fchown(dirfd, uid, gid, ctx);
    68	    }
    69	
    70	    let uid = to_optional_id(uid, Uid::new)?;
    71	    let gid = to_optional_id(gid, Gid::new)?;
    72	    if uid.is_none() && gid.is_none() {
    73	        return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0));
    74	    }
    75	
    76	    let path = {
    77	        let path_name = path_name.to_string_lossy();
    78	        let fs_path =
    79	            FsPath::from_fd_at(dirfd, &path_name, EmptyPathStr::AllowIfFlag(flags.bits()))?;
    80	
    81	        let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
    82	        let path_resolver = fs_ref.resolver().read();
    83	        if flags.contains(ChownFlags::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW) {
    84	            path_resolver.lookup_no_follow(&fs_path)?
    85	        } else {
    86	            path_resolver.lookup(&fs_path)?
    87	        }
    88	    };
    89	
    90	    if let Some(uid) = uid {
    91	        path.set_owner(uid)?;
    92	    }
    93	    if let Some(gid) = gid {
    94	        path.set_group(gid)?;
    95	    }
    96	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    97	}
    98	
    99	fn to_optional_id<T>(id: i32, f: impl Fn(u32) -> T) -> Result<Option<T>> {
   100	    let id = if id >= 0 {
   101	        Some(f(id as u32))
   102	    } else if id == -1 {
   103	        // If the owner or group is specified as -1, then that ID is not changed.
   104	        None
   105	    } else {
   106	        return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL);
   107	    };
   108	
   109	    Ok(id)
   110	}
   111	
   112	bitflags! {
   113	    struct ChownFlags: u32 {
   114	        const AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW = 1 << 8;
   115	        const AT_EMPTY_PATH = 1 << 12;
   116	    }
   117	}

    16	pub fn sys_chdir(path_ptr: Vaddr, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    17	    let path_name = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_ptr, MAX_FILENAME_LEN)?;
    18	    debug!("path = {:?}", path_name);
    19	
    20	    let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
    21	    let mut path_resolver = fs_ref.resolver().write();
    22	    let path = {
    23	        let path_name = path_name.to_string_lossy();
    24	        if path_name.is_empty() {
    25	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOENT, "path is empty");
    26	        }
    27	        let fs_path = FsPath::try_from(path_name.as_ref())?;
    28	        path_resolver.lookup(&fs_path)?
    29	    };
    30	    if path.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
    31	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "must be directory");
    32	    }
    33	    path_resolver.set_cwd(path);
    34	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    35	}
    36	
    37	pub fn sys_fchdir(raw_fd: RawFileDesc, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    38	    debug!("raw_fd = {}", raw_fd);
    39	
    40	    let path = {
    41	        let mut file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table_mut();
    42	        let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, raw_fd.try_into()?);
    43	        file.as_inode_handle_or_err()?.path().clone()
    44	    };
    45	    if path.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
    46	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "must be directory");
    47	    }
    48	    let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
    49	    fs_ref.resolver().write().set_cwd(path);
    50	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    51	}

    21	/// The file descriptor of the current working directory.
    22	pub const AT_FDCWD: RawFileDesc = -100;

    24	/// The `AT_EMPTY_PATH` flag bit, as defined by Linux.
    25	pub const AT_EMPTY_PATH: u32 = 0x1000;
    26	
    27	/// Policy for how [`FsPath::from_fd_at`] treats an empty `path_str`.
    28	///
    29	/// [`FsPath::from_fd_at`] is the entry point that
    30	/// every `*at` syscall uses to resolve its `(dirfd, path_str)` argument pair.
    31	/// Whether an empty `path_str` should be rejected, conditionally accepted,
    32	/// or always accepted depends on the specific syscall's Linux semantics.
    33	/// This enum makes that decision explicit at every call site.
    34	///
    35	/// # Examples
    36	///
    37	/// One call per variant, showing the three patterns side by side:
    38	///
    39	/// ```ignore
    40	/// // mkdirat — dentry op, a name is mandatory.
    41	/// let fs_path = FsPath::from_fd_at(dirfd, &path_str, EmptyPathStr::Reject)?;
    42	///
    43	/// // faccessat2 — inode op with a `flags` argument; `""` accepted
    44	/// // only if the caller passed AT_EMPTY_PATH.
    45	/// let fs_path = FsPath::from_fd_at(
    46	///     dirfd,
    47	///     &path_str,
    48	///     EmptyPathStr::AllowIfFlag(flags.bits()),
    49	/// )?;
    50	///
    51	/// // readlinkat — inode op with no `flags` argument; `""` accepted
    52	/// // only when `dirfd` is a real fd rather than `AT_FDCWD`.
    53	/// let fs_path = FsPath::from_fd_at(dirfd, &path_str, EmptyPathStr::Allow)?;
    54	/// ```
    55	///
    56	/// # Design Rationales
    57	///
    58	/// Two questions about a syscall determine which variant is correct:
    59	///
    60	/// 1. **Target.** Does the syscall operate on an *inode*
    61	///    (the data or metadata behind `dirfd`)
    62	///    or on a *directory entry* (a name inside a parent directory)?
    63	///    An empty `path_str` means "operate on `dirfd` directly" —
    64	///    meaningful only for inode ops,
    65	///    since there is no such thing as a nameless dentry
    66	///    to create, remove, rename, or open.
    67	///
    68	/// 2. **ABI.** Does the syscall carry a `flags` argument
    69	///    that can hold the [`AT_EMPTY_PATH`] opt-in bit?
    70	///    `AT_EMPTY_PATH` post-dates most `*at` syscalls,
    71	///    and callers historically passed `""` by accident (and received `ENOENT`),
    72	///    so the accept-empty behaviour is opt-in —
    73	///    and the opt-in must live in a `flags` word.
    74	///
    75	/// Quick chooser:
    76	///
    77	/// - dentry op → [`Reject`](Self::Reject)
    78	/// - inode op with a `flags` argument → [`AllowIfFlag`](Self::AllowIfFlag)
    79	/// - inode op that accepts `""` without a `flags` argument → [`Allow`](Self::Allow)
    80	///   (rare; see the variant's docs)
    81	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
    82	pub enum EmptyPathStr {
    83	    /// Always reject an empty `path_str` with `ENOENT`; a name is mandatory.
    84	    ///
    85	    /// Use for syscalls whose target is a **directory entry** —
    86	    /// create, remove, rename, or open a named file.
    87	    /// Examples: `openat`, `mkdirat`, `mknodat`, `symlinkat`, `unlinkat`,
    88	    /// `renameat[2]`, and `linkat`'s *newname*.
    89	    ///
    90	    /// Also the right choice for the 3-argument forms
    91	    /// `faccessat`, `fchmodat`, and `futimesat`:
    92	    /// they are inode ops,
    93	    /// but they predate [`AT_EMPTY_PATH`] and
    94	    /// have no `flags` word to carry the opt-in.
    95	    /// Newer code should prefer `faccessat2` / `fchmodat2` with
    96	    /// [`AllowIfFlag`](Self::AllowIfFlag).
    97	    Reject,
    98	
    99	    /// Accept an empty `path_str`
   100	    /// iff [`AT_EMPTY_PATH`] is set in the enclosed raw flag bits;
   101	    /// otherwise behave like [`Reject`](Self::Reject).
   102	    ///
   103	    /// Callers pass their syscall's raw flag bits —
   104	    /// typically `flags.bits()` after parsing the flag word into a typed `BitFlags`.
   105	    /// Only the [`AT_EMPTY_PATH`] bit is inspected; the rest are ignored.
   106	    ///
   107	    /// Use for **inode-target** syscalls that carry a `flags` argument —
   108	    /// the common case for attribute queries and modifications on the inode behind `dirfd`.
   109	    /// Examples: `faccessat2`, `fchmodat2`, `fchownat`, `fstatat` / `newfstatat`,
   110	    /// `statx`, `utimensat`, `name_to_handle_at`, `execveat`, and `linkat`'s *oldname*.
   111	    ///
   112	    /// Any additional gating on top of [`AT_EMPTY_PATH`]
   113	    /// (for instance, `linkat` also requires `CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH`)
   114	    /// stays in the syscall implementation;
   115	    /// this enum only decides whether an empty `path_str` is syntactically acceptable.
   116	    AllowIfFlag(u32),
   117	
   118	    /// Accept an empty `path_str` and operate on `dirfd` directly.
   119	    /// No flag is consulted.
   120	    ///
   121	    /// `dirfd` must be a real file descriptor; if it is [`AT_FDCWD`],
   122	    /// [`FsPath::from_fd_at`] still rejects `""` with `ENOENT`.
   123	    ///
   124	    /// Use only for inode-target syscalls
   125	    /// that Linux has chosen to accept an empty `path_str` unconditionally,
   126	    /// because they have no `flags` argument
   127	    /// in which [`AT_EMPTY_PATH`] could live.
   128	    /// This is deliberately rare:
   129	    /// as of Linux 6.8 the sole such syscall is **`readlinkat`**.
   130	    Allow,
   131	}
   132	
   133	/// A resolver for [`Path`]s.
   134	///
   135	/// `PathResolver` provides a context for resolving paths, defined by a root directory
   136	/// and a current working directory. It handles path resolution across different mount
   137	/// points and within a specific mount namespace.
   138	///
   139	/// All operations related to path resolution for a process should go through its associated
   140	/// `PathResolver`.
   141	#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
   142	pub struct PathResolver {
   143	    root: Path,
   144	    cwd: Path,
   145	}
   500	    /// Converts the `AbsPathResult` into a `String`.
   501	    pub fn into_string(self) -> String {
   502	        match self {
   503	            AbsPathResult::Reachable(s) => s,
   504	            AbsPathResult::Unreachable(s) => s,
   505	        }
   506	    }
   507	}

   509	// Mount info reading implementation
   510	impl PathResolver {
   511	    /// Collects the mounts visible to this resolver.
   512	    ///
   513	    /// Here, the visible mounts are defined as follows:
   514	    /// 1. If the resolver's root is a mount point, the visible mounts are the mount of the
   515	    ///    resolver's root directory and all of its descendant mounts in the mount tree.
   516	    /// 2. If the resolver's root is not a mount point, the visible mounts are all descendant
   517	    ///    mounts that are mounted under the resolver's root directory.
   518	    ///
   519	    /// The mounts are collected in depth-first order.
   520	    pub(in crate::fs) fn collect_visible_mounts(&self) -> Vec<Arc<Mount>> {
   521	        let _topology_guard = MountTopology::read_lock();
   522	        let mut visible = Vec::new();
   523	        let mut stack = vec![self.root.mount.clone()];
   524	        let is_root_mount_root = self.root.is_mount_root();
   525	
   526	        while let Some(mount) = stack.pop() {
   527	            let is_root_mount = Arc::ptr_eq(&mount, &self.root.mount);
   528	
   529	            // Add the root mount only if `self` is at the mount root.
   530	            if !is_root_mount || is_root_mount_root {
   531	                visible.push(mount.clone());
   532	            }
   533	
   534	            let children = mount.children.read();
   535	            for child_mount in children.values() {
   536	                if is_root_mount && !is_root_mount_root {
   537	                    let Some(mountpoint) = child_mount.mountpoint() else {
   538	                        continue;
   539	                    };
   540	                    if !mountpoint.is_equal_or_descendant_of(&self.root.dentry) {
   541	                        continue;
   542	                    }
   543	                }
   544	                stack.push(child_mount.clone());
   545	            }
   546	        }
   547	
   548	        visible
   549	    }
   550	}

   552	// Path lookup implementations
   553	impl PathResolver {
   554	    /// Looks up a child entry with `name` within a directory `path`.
   555	    pub fn lookup_at_path(&self, path: &Path, name: &str) -> Result<Path> {
   556	        let dir_dentry = path.dentry.as_dir_dentry_or_err()?;
   557	
   558	        if path.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_EXEC).is_err() {
   559	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EACCES, "the path cannot be looked up");
   560	        }
   561	        if name.len() > NAME_MAX {
   562	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG, "the path name is too long");
   563	        }
   564	
   565	        let target_path = if super::is_dot(name) {
   566	            return Ok(path.this());
   567	        } else if super::is_dotdot(name) {
   568	            self.resolve_parent(path).unwrap_or_else(|| path.this())
   569	        } else {
   570	            let target_dentry = dir_dentry.lookup_child(name)?;
   571	            Path::new(path.mount.clone(), target_dentry)
   572	        };
   573	
   574	        Ok(target_path.get_top_path())
   575	    }
   576	
   577	    /// Lookups the target `Path` according to the `fs_path`.
   578	    ///
   579	    /// Symlinks are always followed.
   580	    pub fn lookup(&self, fs_path: &FsPath) -> Result<Path> {
   581	        self.lookup_unresolved(fs_path)?.into_path()
   582	    }
   583	
   584	    /// Lookups the target `Path` according to the `fs_path`.
   585	    ///
   870	    pub fn from_fd(dirfd: RawFileDesc) -> Result<Self> {
   871	        let fs_path_inner = if dirfd == AT_FDCWD {
   872	            FsPathInner::Cwd
   873	        } else {
   874	            FsPathInner::Fd(FileDesc::try_from(dirfd)?)
   875	        };
   876	
   877	        Ok(Self {
   878	            inner: fs_path_inner,
   879	        })
   880	    }
   881	
   882	    /// Constructs an `FsPath` from `(dirfd, path_str)`,
   883	    /// applying the syscall-specific empty-path-string `policy`.
   884	    ///
   885	    /// An empty `path_str` is handled per the variant of `policy`;
   886	    /// see [`EmptyPathStr`] for the full rule. A non-empty absolute `path`
   887	    /// ignores `dirfd`; otherwise `dirfd` must be either [`AT_FDCWD`] or a
   888	    /// valid file descriptor.
   889	    ///
   890	    /// # Errors
   891	    ///
   892	    /// - `ENOENT` if `path` is empty and `policy` does not permit it.
   893	    /// - `ENAMETOOLONG` if `path.len() > PATH_MAX`.
   894	    /// - `EBADF` if `dirfd` is negative and is not [`AT_FDCWD`].
   895	    pub fn from_fd_at(dirfd: RawFileDesc, path: &'a str, policy: EmptyPathStr) -> Result<Self> {
   896	        if path.is_empty() {
   897	            let allowed = match policy {
   898	                EmptyPathStr::Reject => false,
   899	                EmptyPathStr::AllowIfFlag(f) => f & AT_EMPTY_PATH != 0,
   900	                EmptyPathStr::Allow => dirfd != AT_FDCWD,
   901	            };
   902	            if !allowed {
   903	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOENT, "the path is empty");
   904	            }
   905	            return Self::from_fd(dirfd);
   906	        }
   907	
   908	        if path.len() > PATH_MAX {
   909	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG, "the path is too long");
   910	        }
   911	
   912	        let fs_path_inner = if path.starts_with('/') {
   913	            FsPathInner::Absolute(path)
   914	        } else if dirfd == AT_FDCWD {
   915	            FsPathInner::CwdRelative(path)
   916	        } else {
   917	            FsPathInner::FdRelative(FileDesc::try_from(dirfd)?, path)
   918	        };
   919	
   920	        Ok(Self {
   921	            inner: fs_path_inner,
   922	        })
   923	    }
   924	}

   389	
   390	    fn page_cache(&self) -> Option<PageCache> {
   530	
   531	    fn set_xattr(
   532	        &self,
   533	        name: XattrName,

   549	        Err(Error::new(Errno::EOPNOTSUPP))
   550	    }
   551	
   552	    /// Used to check for read/write/execute permissions on a file.
   553	    ///
   554	    /// Similar to Linux, using "fsuid" here allows setting filesystem permissions
   555	    /// without changing the "normal" uids for other tasks.
   556	    fn check_permission(&self, mut perm: Permission) -> Result<()> {
   557	        let Some(task) = Task::current() else {
   558	            return Ok(());
   559	        };
   560	        let Some(posix_thread) = task.as_posix_thread() else {
   561	            return Ok(());
   562	        };
   563	
   564	        let creds = posix_thread.credentials();
   565	        let metadata = self.metadata();
   566	        let mode = metadata.mode;
   567	
   568	        // With DAC_OVERRIDE capability, the user can bypass some permission checks.
   569	        if has_dac_override_capability(&task, posix_thread) {
   570	            // Read/write DACs are always overridable.
   571	            perm -= Permission::MAY_READ | Permission::MAY_WRITE;
   572	
   573	            // Executable DACs are overridable when there is at least one exec bit set.
   574	            if perm.may_exec() {
   575	                if mode.is_owner_executable()
   576	                    || mode.is_group_executable()
   577	                    || mode.is_other_executable()
   578	                {
   579	                    perm -= Permission::MAY_EXEC;
   580	                } else {
   581	                    return_errno_with_message!(
   582	                        Errno::EACCES,
   583	                        "root execute permission denied: no execute bits set"
   584	                    );
   585	                }
   586	            }
   587	        }
   588	
   589	        if metadata.uid == creds.fsuid() {
   590	            if (perm.may_read() && !mode.is_owner_readable())
   591	                || (perm.may_write() && !mode.is_owner_writable())
   592	                || (perm.may_exec() && !mode.is_owner_executable())
   593	            {
   594	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EACCES, "owner permission check failed");
   595	            }
   596	        } else if metadata.gid == creds.fsgid() {
   597	            if (perm.may_read() && !mode.is_group_readable())
   598	                || (perm.may_write() && !mode.is_group_writable())
   599	                || (perm.may_exec() && !mode.is_group_executable())
   600	            {
   601	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EACCES, "group permission check failed");
   602	            }
   603	        } else if (perm.may_read() && !mode.is_other_readable())
   604	            || (perm.may_write() && !mode.is_other_writable())
   605	            || (perm.may_exec() && !mode.is_other_executable())
   606	        {
   607	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EACCES, "other permission check failed");
   608	        }
   609	
   610	        Ok(())
   611	    }
   612	}

   623	}

# Comments

### Follow RFC 1574 summary line conventions (`rfc1574-summary`) {#rfc1574-summary}

The first line of a doc comment should be concise and one sentence.
Its grammatical form depends on what the item is:

- **Functions and methods** — third-person singular present indicative verb
  ("Returns", "Creates", "Acquires"), describing the action performed.
- **Types (structs, enums, traits, type aliases), modules, and fields** —
  a noun phrase naming the thing, not describing an action.
  This matches the Rust standard library convention
  (e.g., `Vec` is "A contiguous growable array type").

```rust
/// Returns the mapping's start address.
pub fn map_to_addr(&self) -> Vaddr {
    self.map_to_addr
}

/// A policy for how [`FsPath::from_fd_at`] treats an empty `path_str`.
pub enum EmptyPathStr { /* ... */ }

/// A guard that releases a [`SpinLock`] when dropped.
pub struct SpinLockGuard<'a, T> { /* ... */ }
```

### End sentence comments with punctuation (`comment-punctuation`) {#comment-punctuation}

If a comment line is a full sentence,
end it with proper punctuation.
This improves readability in dense code
and avoids fragmented prose.

```rust
// Good — complete sentence with punctuation.
// SAFETY: The pointer is derived from a live allocation.

// Bad — complete sentence without punctuation
// SAFETY: The pointer is derived from a live allocation
```

### Wrap identifiers in backticks (`backtick-identifiers`) {#backtick-identifiers}

Type names, method names,
and code identifiers in doc comments
should be wrapped in backticks for rustdoc rendering.
When referring to types,
prefer rustdoc links (`[TypeName]`) where possible.

```rust
/// Acquires the [`SpinLock`] and returns a guard
/// that releases the lock on [`Drop`].
///
/// Callers must not call `acquire` while holding
/// a [`RwMutex`] to avoid deadlock.
pub fn acquire(&self) -> SpinLockGuard<'_, T> { ... }
```

### Do not disclose implementation details in doc comments (`no-impl-in-docs`) {#no-impl-in-docs}

Doc comments should describe _what_ the API does
and _how to use it_,
not _how it is implemented internally_.

```rust
// Good — behavior-oriented
/// Returns the number of active connections.

// Bad — leaks implementation details
/// Returns the length of the internal `HashMap`
/// that tracks connections by socket address.
```
// Bad — the reader must infer why this bit is special.
let needs_ack = (packet_flags & 0b0000_0100) != 0;
```

Prefer deriving related values from the named source
instead of repeating the same number in multiple places.
If the name alone does not explain where the value comes from,
add a short comment or cite the relevant specification.

Do not introduce names for numbers
whose meaning is already obvious locally,
such as `0`, `1`, or `2`
in ordinary arithmetic,
indexing,
small ranges,
or direct comparisons.

### Use assertion-style boolean names (`bool-names`) {#bool-names}

Boolean variables and functions
should read as assertions of fact.
Use `is_`, `has_`, `can_`, `should_`, `was_`,
or `needs_` prefixes.
Never use negated names
(`is_not_empty`, `no_error`);
prefer the positive form
(`is_empty`, `ok` or `succeeded`).
A bare name like `found`, `done`, or `ready`
is acceptable when the context is unambiguous.

```rust
// Good — reads as an assertion
fn is_page_aligned(&self) -> bool { ... }
fn has_permission(&self, perm: Permission) -> bool { ... }
let can_read = mode.is_readable();

// Bad — verb suggests an action, not a query
fn check_permission(&self, perm: Permission) -> bool { ... }
// Bad — negated name
let is_not_empty = !buf.is_empty();
```

See also:
PR [#1488](https://github.com/asterinas/asterinas/pull/1488#discussion_r1841827039).

### Format error messages consistently (`error-message-format`) {#error-message-format}

Start with a lowercase letter
(unless the first word is a proper noun or identifier).
Be specific:
prefer "`len` is too large" over "the argument is invalid".

For system call errors,
follow the style and descriptions in Linux man pages.

   615	    fn lookup_inner(&self, fs_path: &FsPath, follow_tail_link: bool) -> Result<LookupResult> {
   616	        let lookup_res = match fs_path.inner {
   617	            FsPathInner::Absolute(path) => {
   618	                self.lookup_from_parent(&self.root, path.trim_start_matches('/'), follow_tail_link)?
   619	            }
   620	            FsPathInner::CwdRelative(path) => {
   621	                self.lookup_from_parent(&self.cwd, path, follow_tail_link)?
   622	            }
   623	            FsPathInner::Cwd => LookupResult::Resolved(self.cwd.clone()),
   624	            FsPathInner::FdRelative(fd, path) => {
   625	                let parent = {
   626	                    let task = Task::current().unwrap();
   627	                    let mut file_table = task.as_thread_local().unwrap().borrow_file_table_mut();
   628	                    let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, fd);
   629	                    file.path().clone()
   630	                };
   631	                self.lookup_from_parent(&parent, path, follow_tail_link)?
   632	            }
   633	            FsPathInner::Fd(fd) => {
   634	                let task = Task::current().unwrap();
   635	                let mut file_table = task.as_thread_local().unwrap().borrow_file_table_mut();
   636	                let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, fd);
   637	                LookupResult::Resolved(file.path().clone())
   638	            }
   639	        };

   644	    /// Lookups the target path according to the parent directory path.
   645	    ///
   646	    /// The length of `path` cannot exceed `PATH_MAX`.
   647	    /// If `path` ends with `/`, then the returned inode must be a directory inode.
   648	    ///
   649	    /// While looking up the path, symbolic links will be followed for
   650	    /// at most `SYMLINKS_MAX` times.
   651	    ///
   652	    /// If `follow_tail_link` is true and the trailing component is a symlink,
   653	    /// it will be followed.
   654	    /// Symlinks in earlier components of the path will always be followed.
   655	    #[expect(clippy::redundant_closure)]
   656	    fn lookup_from_parent(
   657	        &self,
   658	        parent: &Path,
   659	        relative_path: &str,
   660	        follow_tail_link: bool,
   661	    ) -> Result<LookupResult> {
   662	        debug_assert!(!relative_path.starts_with('/'));
   663	
   664	        if relative_path.len() > PATH_MAX {
   665	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG, "the path is too long");
   666	        }
   667	        if relative_path.is_empty() {
   668	            return Ok(LookupResult::Resolved(parent.clone()));
   669	        }
   670	
   671	        // To handle symlinks
   672	        let mut link_path_opt = None;
   673	        let mut follows = 0;
   674	
   675	        // Initialize the first path and the relative path
   676	        let (mut current_path, mut relative_path) = (parent.clone(), relative_path);
   677	
   678	        while !relative_path.is_empty() {
   679	            let (next_name, path_remain, target_is_dir) =
   680	                if let Some((prefix, suffix)) = relative_path.split_once('/') {
   681	                    let suffix = suffix.trim_start_matches('/');
   682	                    (prefix, suffix, true)
   683	                } else {
   684	                    (relative_path, "", false)
   685	                };
   686	
   687	            // Iterate next path
   688	            let next_is_tail = path_remain.is_empty();
   689	            let next_path = match self.lookup_at_path(&current_path, next_name) {
   690	                Ok(child) => child,
   691	                Err(e) => {
   692	                    if next_is_tail && e.error() == Errno::ENOENT {
   693	                        return Ok(LookupResult::AtParent(LookupParentResult::new(
   694	                            current_path,
   695	                            next_name.to_string(),
   696	                            target_is_dir,
   697	                        )));
   698	                    }
   699	                    return Err(e);
   700	                }
   701	            };
   702	
   703	            let next_type = next_path.type_();
   704	            // If next inode is a symlink, follow symlinks at most `SYMLINKS_MAX` times.
   705	            if next_type == InodeType::SymLink
   706	                && (follow_tail_link || !next_is_tail
   707	                    // A trailing `/` forces directory semantics, so the tail symlink must still be
   708	                    // resolved even when `follow_tail_link` is false.
   709	                    || target_is_dir)
   710	            {
   711	                if follows >= SYMLINKS_MAX {
   712	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ELOOP, "there are too many symlinks");
   713	                }
   714	                let read_link_res = next_path.inode().read_link()?;
   715	                match read_link_res {
   716	                    SymbolicLink::Plain(mut tmp_link_path) => {
   717	                        let link_path_remain = {
   718	                            if tmp_link_path.is_empty() {
   719	                                return_errno_with_message!(
   720	                                    Errno::ENOENT,
   721	                                    "the symlink path is empty"
   722	                                );
   723	                            }
   724	                            if !next_is_tail {
   725	                                tmp_link_path += "/";
   726	                                tmp_link_path += path_remain;
   727	                            } else if target_is_dir {
   728	                                tmp_link_path += "/";
   729	                            }
   730	                            tmp_link_path
   731	                        };
   732	
   733	                        // Change the path and relative path according to symlink
   734	                        if link_path_remain.starts_with('/') {
   735	                            current_path = self.root.clone();
   736	                        }
   737	                        let link_path = link_path_opt.get_or_insert_with(|| String::new());
   738	                        link_path.clear();
   739	                        link_path.push_str(link_path_remain.trim_start_matches('/'));
   740	                        relative_path = link_path;
   741	                        follows += 1;
   742	                    }
   743	                    SymbolicLink::Path(path) => {
   744	                        current_path = path;
   745	                        relative_path = path_remain;
   746	                        follows += 1;
   747	                    }
   748	                }
   749	            } else {
   750	                // If path ends with `/`, the inode must be a directory
   751	                if target_is_dir && next_type != InodeType::Dir {
   752	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "the inode is not a directory");
   753	                }
   754	                current_path = next_path;
   755	                relative_path = path_remain;
   756	            }
   757	        }
   758	
   759	        Ok(LookupResult::Resolved(current_path))
   760	    }

   393	/// Returns the file's path if open/close events should be emitted for it.
   394	///
   395	/// `O_PATH` file descriptors carry `FMODE_NONOTIFY` in Linux's `f_mode` and
   396	/// thus suppress all fsnotify events, so they yield `None`. `None` is also
   397	/// returned when the filesystem has no event subscribers.
   398	fn notifiable_path(file: &Arc<dyn FileLike>) -> Option<&Path> {
   399	    if file.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_PATH) {
   400	        return None;
   401	    }
   402	    let path = file.path();
   403	    if !path.fs().fs_event_subscriber_stats().has_any_subscribers() {
   404	        return None;
   405	    }
   406	    Some(path)
   407	}
   408	
   409	/// Notifies that a file's attributes changed.
   410	pub fn on_attr_change(path: &Path) {
   411	    if !path.fs().fs_event_subscriber_stats().has_any_subscribers() {
   412	        return;
   413	    }
   414	    notify_parent(path, FsEvents::ATTRIB);
   415	}
   416	
   417	/// Notifies a path's parent and the path itself about filesystem events.
   418	///
   419	/// If the parent is watching or if subscribers have registered interesting events with
   420	/// parent and name information, notifies the parent with child name info.
   421	/// Otherwise, notifies only the child without name information.
   422	/// This function is already called after filesystem checking in the callers.
   423	///
   424	/// The child's real name (from `path.name()`) is used to notify the parent, since
   425	/// FS events do not cross mount boundaries.
   426	fn notify_parent(path: &Path, mut events: FsEvents) {
   427	    if path.inode().type_() == InodeType::Dir {
   428	        events |= FsEvents::ISDIR;
   429	    }

    49	    struct FaccessatFlags: u32 {
    50	        const AT_EACCESS = 0x200;
    51	        const AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW = 0x100;
    52	        const AT_EMPTY_PATH = 0x1000;

    56	bitflags! {
    57	    struct AccessMode: u32 {
    58	        const R_OK = 0x4;
    59	        const W_OK = 0x2;
    60	        const X_OK = 0x1;
    61	        // We should ignore F_OK in bitflags.
    62	        // const F_OK = 0x0;
    63	    }

    65	
    66	fn do_faccessat(
    67	    dirfd: RawFileDesc,
    68	    path_ptr: Vaddr,
    69	    mode: u32,
    70	    flags: u32,
    71	    ctx: &Context,
    72	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    73	    let mode = AccessMode::from_bits(mode)
    74	        .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid mode"))?;
    75	    let flags = FaccessatFlags::from_bits(flags)
    76	        .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid flags"))?;
    77	
    78	    let path_name = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_ptr, PATH_MAX)?;
    79	    debug!(
    80	        "dirfd = {}, path_name = {:?}, mode = {:o}, flags = {:?}",
    81	        dirfd, path_name, mode, flags
    82	    );
    83	
    84	    let path = {
    85	        let path_name = path_name.to_string_lossy();
    86	        let fs_path =
    87	            FsPath::from_fd_at(dirfd, &path_name, EmptyPathStr::AllowIfFlag(flags.bits()))?;
    88	
    89	        let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
    90	        let path_resolver = fs_ref.resolver().read();
    91	        if flags.contains(FaccessatFlags::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW) {
    92	            path_resolver.lookup_no_follow(&fs_path)?
    93	        } else {
    94	            path_resolver.lookup(&fs_path)?
    95	        }
    96	    };
    97	
    98	    let inode = path.inode();
    99	
   100	    // F_OK is represented by `AccessMode::empty()`, which does not perform permission checks.
   101	    if mode.contains(AccessMode::R_OK) {
   102	        inode.check_permission(Permission::MAY_READ)?;
   103	    }
   104	    if mode.contains(AccessMode::W_OK) {
   105	        inode.check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)?;
   106	    }
   107	    if mode.contains(AccessMode::X_OK) {
   108	        inode.check_permission(Permission::MAY_EXEC)?;
   109	    }
   110	
   111	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
   112	}

   939	    /// `basename()`](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/basename.3.html). Trailing slashes
   940	    /// (`/`) are trimmed from returned names unless the name refers to the root directory.
   941	    ///
   942	    /// If the original path directly points to the root directory (e.g., `/` or `//`, but not `/.`

     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
     4	
     5	use atomic_integer_wrapper::define_atomic_version_of_integer_like_type;
     6	
     7	use crate::prelude::*;
     8	
     9	#[repr(C)]
    10	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq, Pod)]
    11	pub struct Uid(u32);
    12	
    13	const ROOT_UID: u32 = 0;
    14	
    15	impl Uid {
    16	    /// The invalid UID, typically used to indicate that no valid UID is found when returning to user space.
    17	    ///
    18	    /// Reference: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.15/source/include/linux/uidgid.h#L50>.
    19	    pub const INVALID: Uid = Self::new(u32::MAX);
    20	
    21	    /// The overflow UID, typically used to indicate that user mappings between namespaces fail.
    22	    ///
    23	    /// This is currently a constant (65534 is usually the "nobody" user), but it should be
    24	    /// configured via `/proc/sys/kernel/overflowuid`.
    25	    ///
    26	    /// Reference: <https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.15/source/kernel/sys.c#L166>.
    27	    pub const OVERFLOW: Uid = Self::new(65534);
    28	
    29	    pub const fn new_root() -> Self {
    30	        Self(ROOT_UID)
    31	    }
    32	
    33	    pub const fn new(uid: u32) -> Self {
    34	        Self(uid)
    35	    }
    36	
    37	    pub const fn is_root(&self) -> bool {
    38	        self.0 == ROOT_UID
    39	    }
    40	}
    41	
    42	impl From<u32> for Uid {
    43	    fn from(value: u32) -> Self {
    44	        Self::new(value)
    45	    }
    46	}
    47	
    48	impl From<Uid> for u32 {
    49	    fn from(value: Uid) -> Self {
    50	        value.0
    51	    }
    52	}
    53	
    54	define_atomic_version_of_integer_like_type!(Uid, {
    55	    #[derive(Debug)]
    56	    pub(super) struct AtomicUid(AtomicU32);
    57	});
    58	
    59	impl Clone for AtomicUid {
    60	    fn clone(&self) -> Self {
    61	        Self::new(self.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
    62	    }
    63	}

codex
Local Linux behavior confirms that `fchownat(fd, NULL, ..., AT_EMPTY_PATH)` returns `EFAULT`, while `fchownat(fd, "", ..., AT_EMPTY_PATH)` succeeds. I’ll retract that specific correctness candidate and keep the empty-string path handling findings separate.

   199	/// This macro is used to define syscall handler.
   200	/// The first param is the number of parameters,
   201	/// The second param is the function name of syscall handler,
   202	/// The third is optional, means the args(if parameter number > 0),
   203	/// The fourth is optional, means if cpu ctx is required.
   204	macro_rules! syscall_handler {
   205	    (0, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr) => {
   206	        $fn_name($ctx)
   207	    };
   208	    (0, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr, $user_ctx: expr) => {
   209	        $fn_name($ctx, $user_ctx)
   210	    };
   211	
   212	    (1, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr) => {
   213	        $fn_name($args[0] as _, $ctx)
   214	    };
   215	    (1, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr, $user_ctx: expr) => {
   216	        $fn_name($args[0] as _, $ctx, $user_ctx)
   217	    };
   218	
   219	    (2, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr) => {
   220	        $fn_name($args[0] as _, $args[1] as _, $ctx)
   221	    };
   222	    (2, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr, $user_ctx: expr) => {
   223	        $fn_name($args[0] as _, $args[1] as _, $ctx, $user_ctx)
   224	    };
   225	
   226	    (3, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr) => {
   227	        $fn_name($args[0] as _, $args[1] as _, $args[2] as _, $ctx)
   228	    };
   229	    (3, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr, $user_ctx: expr) => {
   230	        $fn_name($args[0] as _, $args[1] as _, $args[2] as _, $ctx, $user_ctx)
   231	    };
   232	
   233	    (4, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr) => {
   234	        $fn_name(
   235	            $args[0] as _,
   236	            $args[1] as _,
   237	            $args[2] as _,
   238	            $args[3] as _,
   239	            $ctx,
   240	        )
   241	    };
   242	    (4, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr, $user_ctx: expr) => {
   243	        $fn_name(
   244	            $args[0] as _,
   245	            $args[1] as _,
   246	            $args[2] as _,
   247	            $args[3] as _,
   248	            $ctx,
   249	            $user_ctx,
   250	        )
   251	    };
   252	
   253	    (5, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr) => {
   254	        $fn_name(
   255	            $args[0] as _,
   256	            $args[1] as _,
   257	            $args[2] as _,
   258	            $args[3] as _,
   259	            $args[4] as _,
   260	            $ctx,
   261	        )
   262	    };
   263	    (5, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr, $user_ctx: expr) => {
   264	        $fn_name(
   265	            $args[0] as _,
   266	            $args[1] as _,
   267	            $args[2] as _,
   268	            $args[3] as _,
   269	            $args[4] as _,
   270	            $ctx,
   271	            $user_ctx,
   272	        )
   273	    };
   274	
   275	    (6, $fn_name: ident, $args: ident, $ctx: expr) => {
   276	        $fn_name(
   277	            $args[0] as _,
   278	            $args[1] as _,
   279	            $args[2] as _,
   280	            $args[3] as _,
   300	macro_rules! dispatch_fn_inner {
   301	    ( $args: ident, $ctx: ident, $user_ctx: ident, $handler: ident ( args[ .. $cnt: tt ] ) ) => {
   302	        $crate::syscall::syscall_handler!($cnt, $handler, $args, $ctx)
   303	    };
   304	    ( $args: ident, $ctx: ident, $user_ctx: ident, $handler: ident ( args[ .. $cnt: tt ] , &user_ctx ) ) => {
   305	        $crate::syscall::syscall_handler!($cnt, $handler, $args, $ctx, &$user_ctx)
   306	    };
   307	    ( $args: ident, $ctx: ident, $user_ctx: ident, $handler: ident ( args[ .. $cnt: tt ] , &mut user_ctx ) ) => {
   308	        // `$user_ctx` is already of type `&mut ostd::cpu::UserContext`,
   309	        // so no need to take `&mut` again
   310	        $crate::syscall::syscall_handler!($cnt, $handler, $args, $ctx, $user_ctx)
   311	    };
   312	}
   313	
   314	macro_rules! impl_syscall_nums_and_dispatch_fn {
   315	    // $args, $user_ctx, and $dispatcher_name are needed since Rust macro is hygienic
   316	    ( $( $name: ident = $num: literal => $handler: ident $args: tt );* $(;)? ) => {
   317	        // First, define the syscall numbers
   318	        $(
   319	            pub const $name: u64 = $num;
   320	        )*
   321	
   322	        // Then, define the dispatcher function
   323	        pub fn syscall_dispatch(
   324	            syscall_number: u64,
   325	            args: [u64; 6],
   326	            ctx: &crate::context::Context,
   327	            user_ctx: &mut ostd::arch::cpu::context::UserContext,
   328	        ) -> $crate::prelude::Result<$crate::syscall::SyscallReturn> {
   329	            match syscall_number {
   330	                $(
   331	                    $num => {
   332	                        $crate::syscall::log_syscall_entry!($name);
   333	                        $crate::syscall::dispatch_fn_inner!(args, ctx, user_ctx, $handler $args)
   334	                    }
   335	                )*
   336	                _ => {
   337	                    ostd::warn!("Unimplemented syscall number: {}", syscall_number);
   338	                    $crate::error::return_errno_with_message!(
   339	                        $crate::error::Errno::ENOSYS,
   340	                        "Syscall was unimplemented"
   341	                    );
   342	                }
   343	            }
   344	        }
   345	    }

codex
The signed-ID finding is confirmed by the syscall dispatch path: raw `u64` arguments are cast to the handler’s declared parameter type, and `Uid`/`Gid` are `u32` wrappers. A user-supplied `0x80000000` would arrive as a negative `i32` in the current handler.
exec
/bin/bash -lc 'TMP=/tmp/chown-high-review-$$; touch "$TMP"; python3 - <<'"'PY' \""'$TMP"
import os, sys, ctypes, errno
path=sys.argv[1]
libc=ctypes.CDLL(None, use_errno=True)
ctypes.set_errno(0)
ret=libc.chown(path.encode(), ctypes.c_uint(0x80000000).value, ctypes.c_uint(-1).value)
err=ctypes.get_errno()
print(ret, err, errno.errorcode.get(err))
try:
    st=os.stat(path); print(st.st_uid)
except Exception as e: print(e)
